  - `models-convert-llama-handmade.txt`: `models convert Llama3.2-1B-Instruct/safetensors/edaba290ce7f --register-with Q4_K_M`, as `run_chain` prints a chain with no job stream (`model_chain.cpp`, `models_pull.cpp`'s convert, quantize and registration lines), its two stored paths the store world's recorded ones, so `models info <path>` answers with `store-info-f16.json` and `store-info-q4.json`;
  - `models-quantize-llama-handmade.txt`: `models quantize <the F16>` at its default Q4_K_M, which the store holds already ("already quantized:", then the backend hint, as `models_pull.cpp` prints them);
  - `delete-plan-llama-q4-handmade.txt`, `delete-kept-llama-q4-handmade.txt`, `delete-and-backends-llama-q4-handmade.txt`, and the same three for the whole model (`-llama-model-`): `delete-plan-backend.txt`'s, `delete-kept.txt`'s and `delete-and-backends.txt`'s lines with the store world's folders and backend;
  - `config-scan-register-handmade.txt` (`config-scan.txt`'s report, then the two registrations in `config-scan-kinds-register.txt`'s shape); `config-add-backend-handmade.txt`, `config-delete-backend-handmade.txt`, `config-set-role-handmade.txt` and `config-set-backend-adapters-handmade.txt`: one line an answer, as `config_cmd.cpp` and `add_backend` print them (`models.<field> = <name>`; the pin's sha256 the set's folder id, as the CLI names a folder by its hash), each line cut into its own rule -- `plain`'s removal answers with the CLI's second line too, `models.default cleared: it named 'plain'` (`remove_backend` clears each role pointer that names the entry, M33; the shape `delete-backend-clears.txt` records), since this config's `models.default` is `plain`, and the role answers take no role the CLI refuses (no `models.default_embedding` on the three `mlx` backends, which chat: `role_refusal`, M33);
  - `fixtures/business/jobs/models-pull-llama-handmade.jsonl` (54 lines): `models pull meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct --safetensors --register-with Q4_K_M --output-format stream-json` -- the chain's four stages, the snapshot's nine files' `bytes` (its total `store-info-snapshot-converts.json`'s), the notices as the chain prints them, then three `document` events, each the recording of that set's `models info` (`store-info-snapshot-converts.json`, `store-info-f16.json`, `store-info-q4.json`) named by its handle. The `fresh` world also answers the conversion its chain names for resuming once the snapshot is stored, with `models-convert-llama-handmade.txt`, that exact command's output.
- [x] **`lib/design/main.html`**, rebuilt on the final tree (its layer cards are build inputs): `dist/main.html 1,116.69 kB │ gzip: 398.78 kB` (was 917.55 kB).

Decisions the item recorded before the build:
- 2026-11-14: **The user's call:** the prototype is rebuilt in React, TypeScript and Vite, with the production GUIs' four layers and MVVM modules, so a new app ports from it; it builds to one self-contained `lib/design/main.html`; a design-queue item in v0.2.1.
- 2026-11-14: **The user's call:** parity screenshots are taken by Playwright in 1s, not here.
- 2026-11-14: **The recorded default:** the data is the Mac app's recorded fixtures, read in place; an unrecorded state gets a `*-handmade*` file in the CLI's shape. View-models are plain TypeScript.
- 2026-11-14: **The recorded default:** parity with the frozen old prototype is judged on layout, states, controls and wording, not pixels or data.
- 2026-11-14: The split: Models is item 1h, gated on 1e, one module per item.
- (consumed) 1d: where hand-made files live, and the preset type. 1e: the confirm and sheet hosts, the toast, and routing.

The open calls' defaults, taken as stated (2026-11-18, the user's answers before the run):
- **The six view-model names are the Mac's** (`ModelsViewModel`, `StoreViewModel`, `DownloadsViewModel`, `StoreForms`, `DeletePlan`, `BackendAdapters`); Roles stays inside `ModelsViewModel`.
- **The jobs live in the business layer's jobs service**, the Mac's `BusinessJobs` (`ChildRegistry`, `JobProgress`, below), and `DownloadsViewModel` presents them. A finished job's effects -- the sets added, the backends registered, the toast -- come from what the run itself reports (a pull's `document` events and its `registered` lines, a store job's printed sets placed by `models info`), never from the clock.
- **`download` opens the filled-in sheet, as the old prototype does.** `pull-chain-done-handmade.jsonl` carries no `bytes` and no `document` (the readiness note's finding), so Start download replays a new hand-made stream with both, `models-pull-llama-handmade.jsonl`; `models:downloads`' Llama conversion and `models:notran`'s quantization run on hand-made output in the CLI's shape, named above.
- **No "Apogee isn't installed" or "config could not be read" state for Models**, mirroring the prototype: with no binary, or a config the CLI refuses, the tables are empty and say the prototype's "Nothing here yet."; the Mac's `.missing` and `models-list-bad-config.err` are a follow-up item's.

Decisions taken while building (2026-11-19, an unattended group run), each for veto:
- **The user's answers before the run:** the npm registry and Playwright's Chromium were allowed; neither was needed (`package.json` and the lock file unchanged; `~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright` still holds `chromium-1248`, `chromium_headless_shell-1248` and `ffmpeg-1013`). No capture was needed and no `apogee` binary ran; no model was pulled, converted or quantized anywhere. A Playwright test opening the built file from `file://` stood in for your look. **You try the file afterwards** (Not done).
- **Three worlds, one store each** (`ModelsWorlds.ts`), so each preset's store is one recording's: `store` (`store-list.json`: Llama 3.2 1B converted and its Q4 registered as `llama3.2-1b-Q4KM`, Gemma 4 26B as MLX, Kokoro) for `models`, `models:roles`, `models:register`, `models:delete` and `models:notran`; `conversions` (`store-list-conversions.json`: the snapshots before any conversion) for `models:store`, `models:convert` and `models:downloads`; `fresh` (the same without the Llama snapshot) for `download`. Each is joined with the sessions' backends and the drafters' recordings, so the sidebar's glyphs and the session behind keep their facts and the DRAFTER section has a base to pair; the joined lists are those recorded rows, merged in the CLI's order (the hand-made lists above). The session behind a Models preset reads the joined list, so its model menu lists the store's backends too on those presets only.
- **The Models place opens whole from every screen:** every preset carries its reads beside its own (`modelsPlaceRules()`, the preset's own answer of the same arguments winning), and the per-preset walk now calls `models.appeared()` on every preset in all three modes, so a click on Models anywhere leaves nothing unanswered.
- **The shared job list is the Mac's `ChildRegistry`, in `business/jobs`** (the readiness note's unowned seam): the job starter registers each job it starts, labelled by its command, and the job leaves at its end; any place reads it without importing `models`. **The sidebar's Models badge reads it** (the readiness note's dangling hand-off: the old prototype has no sidebar job rows, only the place's running count, `main.html:5386-5389`): `AppViewModel.placeRuns.models` counts the registry's `apogee models pull|convert|quantize|repair` jobs, through `ApogeeApp` into the `Sidebar`'s existing `running` prop. The console's job starter shares the registry, so a console `models pull` counts, and its `models list` does not.
- **`JobProgress` is ported as far as a pull and a store job read it**; the training run's iterations and passes and the media events are the items that port those places.
- **What a job made lands from its own report, beside the store's read:** the stand-in keeps no state, so `StoreViewModel.land` keeps each set a job reported -- a pull's documents, a conversion's named sets, a quantization's GGUF -- with the backend its run registered, paired in the order the CLI names both (each runnable set, the next `registered <name>`); a SafeTensors set a reported conversion names in its lineage folds away as `models list` folds it. Commands -- a delete, a registration, a removal, a rename, a role, adapters, a pairing -- read back unchanged and say what the CLI printed (1g's precedent): a deleted set stays listed, its narration card kept.
- **The pull is `meta-llama/Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct`**, the repository the store's recordings pulled, so its documents are recordings; the `download` preset opens on it where the old frame typed `Wan-AI/Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B-Diffusers`. The CLI states nothing of a repository before its pull, so the sheet cannot say the old media branch's words (a diffusion pipeline, a voice model) before the run: the sheet always draws the model form, its Try examples are the old three, and the gap goes to 1r. The CLI's pull makes no MLX model, so the MLX choice pulls the snapshot alone (`--safetensors`), its level menu gone and its summary saying to convert it under Stored weights once it lands.
- **`models:notran` quantizes at Q4_K_M** where the old frame quantized at Q8_0, so Run again replays recorded data (the store holds that quantization; its registration answers with `quantize-add-backend-llama.txt`, recorded word for word); a Q8_0 run is no recording's.
- **`models:delete` deletes the store world's Q4** (`8d51e6c04a27`, its backend `llama3.2-1b-Q4KM`), its plan hand-made in `delete-plan-backend.txt`'s shape; the document's starting point `delete-plan-gemma.txt` belongs to a store with no SafeTensors, MLX or unregistered GGUF, which the other presets need.
- **The stand-in's `exact` rule** (an infrastructure change, test-first): the pull, the conversion and the quantization are recorded for their exact arguments, so a sheet's other choices are no recording's and say so, never replaying another run's output.
- **1g's hand-off, landed:** the inspector's Open in library opens Backends with the session's model selected, and Change helpers opens Roles (`openModels(tab, model)`); the session header's model menu's library door opens Backends at its model. A model the list does not name opens Backends with nothing selected.
- **Use in the open session and Use in a new session set the open session's model** (`SessionViewModel.pickModel`, a `set` line, held while a turn runs) and show Sessions, as the old `pickModelFor` does; the Mac opens a new session.
- **The Models presets keep the Streaming session behind them at its starting frame**, as the old frames keep `taskgroup` open; the sidebar draws no current row off Sessions (1g's rule).
- **`ModelGlyph` and `kindColor` moved to `styles/Components/`** on their second use; `SessionExtras.tsx` re-exports them.
- **`ModelsViewModel` holds and wires the place's other two view-models**, where the Mac's composition builds and wires each: the composition then needs one place handle, as for the other places.
- **Where the port follows the prototype and the Mac differs** (each named, as the document asks):
  - Backends is the prototype's table -- two sections by where a backend runs, ON THIS MAC and CLOUD AND SUBSCRIPTIONS, with ON DISK, WINDOW and MEMORY from the set's document -- where the Mac groups by the CLI's groups (GGUF, MLX, Anthropic …); a row's format cell names the group where it has no format (Anthropic, Claude Code, Other).
  - The details are the prototype's FACTS, DRAFTER, ADAPTERS, LINEAGE and FILES, filled from `models info` (local: Backend, Format, On disk, Window, Memory, Runs on, Path, Weights; API: Provider, Billing, Key, Window, Data -- `sent to the <type> API`, the CLI's type word; subscription: Through, Billing, Usage, Window), where the Mac draws the whole record; LINEAGE is the CLI's `lineage` lines, each labelled by the set it describes. Under the actions, the prototype's note in its precedence: a drafter that cannot draft, in the warning color, else the CLI's own note on the row (`no API key found; run 'apogee auth add anthropic'`, the `mlx` backends' missing Python environment) in secondary text.
  - No backend rename on Backends (the Mac renames a backend or a model there); renaming is Stored weights' model folder, the prototype's.
  - Register all asks with the handles it registers and the command, the prototype's confirm; the Mac shows `config scan`'s report first. `config scan` is read for the register sheet's name.
  - A delete's two doors are the prototype's two buttons; the Mac's is a toggle.
  - A drafter's set offers no Pair with its base or Unpair in Stored weights, and an MLX embedding set no Use for embeddings (the Mac's 3b and 53h): the prototype pairs from the backend's DRAFTER section.
  - The Jobs cards are the prototype's -- the steps' bars, Stop, Resume from …, Show the weights, Run again and Dismiss, and the log (the command, then the run's own lines) -- but for a finished pull's button: Show the weights, where the old says Show the backend (below); the Mac's elapsed time is not drawn ("working").
  - The download sheet is the prototype's form (repository, GGUF or MLX, the level), mapped to the CLI's one pull; the Mac's is a reference, a convert toggle and levels.
  - A role's picker keeps the config's own choice for it, as the prototype keeps its pickers (found in comparison, below): Chat its `models.default`, read from the CLI's own `(via models.default)` line (below), and any other role Not set where a suite or `models.default` fills it, its note saying who fills it in the CLI's words. The Mac reads Not set for every role a suite or `models.default` fills, Chat among them (`ModelsScreen.swift:523`). Not set cannot be chosen: no verb unsets a role. The pickers offer what the CLI lets each role name (below), where the Mac offers every backend.
  - No missing-CLI state, as above.
- **The prototype's words where the CLI says nothing, the CLI's where it does:** status and state are the CLI's words (`ok`, `no key`, `installed`, `no environment`, `-`) where the old drew `ready`, `loaded`, `key set · billed`; a role's note is the CLI's (`fastroot (via suite fast)`, `(unset -- the chat's own backend)`); the header's store line is the sets' stated total, with no free space (no read of the volume here); the sheets' notes drop "412 GB is free"; a SafeTensors set's note drops "whether the converter takes … is known only by converting" where `models info` states what it converts to (`converts_to`), and says nothing where it converts to nothing (the record's Format says why). One new line: an empty Jobs tab says "Nothing has run here yet." (the old tab was never empty).
- **1g's console test waits for the window before its key press:** under this item's live runs in the same suite, ⇧⌘K once landed before React attached its listener (1e's recorded lesson), failing the test once in the full gate; it passed alone three times.
- **The document's own decisions were recorded here, not in it:** it is deleted in the same pass.

Decisions taken in the review's fixes (2026-11-20, the same run), each for veto:
- **No binary, no job** (the Mac's `canStart`, `binary != nil && formArguments != nil`, and its `guard let binary` before a card is made): with no `apogee`, Start download is greyed and does nothing, Jobs is not shown, and a pull or store job started any other way adds no card. The port had added the card first, so it stood "running" with a Stop that could not end it (found in review).
- **Resume from … on a pull runs the store verb its chain names as the store job it is.** A pull stopped after its snapshot names `models convert Llama3.2-1B-Instruct/safetensors/edaba290ce7f --register-with Q4_K_M` (its notice "from here on, if this stops, resume with:"). The port runs it with the store read first and its sets placed as its own output names them, so the F16 and the Q4 land with their backends; the `fresh` world answers it with the conversion's hand-made output, that command's own. The Mac runs it as the pull's command and lands it through its store's refresh, which under a stand-in that keeps no state would land nothing. The stopped card keeps its Resume, as the Mac's does.
- **The done toast is the old prototype's** (`main.html:2891` and 3707-3725 at `b375fac9`), said once a job ends well and never after a Stop, its words from what the run reported: a conversion's `<model>: <n> sets made, <k> registered` (the sets its output named, the backends its `registered` lines name); a quantization's `<model> · <level> is ready in the library` (the level its made set's lineage states); a check's `<handle>: <its last line>`; a pull's `<model> · <level> is ready in the library`, the model the folder its `document` events name and the level the newest quantized set's lineage states, **else the card's own level** (F16, or the MLX choice's snapshot), since a converted F16's document states no precision: that fallback is the sheet's choice, not the run's report.
- **A finished pull's card offers Show the weights, where the old's GGUF or MLX pull says Show the backend** (`main.html:5846`, 5863) and opens Backends at the new backend. The stand-in keeps no state, so Backends' `models list` (`models-list-fresh-handmade.json`) never lists the backend a pull registered, while Stored weights holds the landed sets with their backends: Show the weights opens Stored weights at the first set with a registered backend.
- **The role pickers offer what the CLI lets each role name** (its `role_refusal`, M33): the embedding role what embeds, every other role what chats, an `mlx` entry judged by the kind `models list` states, and the role's current backend always keeps its place; the Mac offers every backend. The old prototype filters finer (vision what sees, transcription what hears, chat and its helpers no API backend without a key). Its vision and hearing filters wait for a read the CLI does not give (no `models list` row states what a backend sees or hears); its no-key filter is not taken, since the CLI points a role at a backend with no key (`role_refusal` judges only an `mlx` entry's kind) and the row's state says `no key`.
- **The Chat picker keeps `models.default`, as the old one does** (`main.html:5914`), read from the CLI's own words on a role that falls back to it (`plain   (via models.default)` in the recorded `models status`), so Chat reads `plain` under suite `fast`. Where no role names `models.default` that way, Chat reads Not set, an intended difference: no `config get models.default` is recorded.

**Side by side with the frozen old prototype** (1c's nine frames at `installed`, against Playwright shots of the built file at 1440 × 900, still). Every region, control and state of the old place is drawn: the header's four tabs with their counts, the store line and Download from Hugging Face…; Backends' table, sections, row glyphs, roles line, drafter mark and the details pane with every section; Stored weights' summary, Register all, the grouped sets with their made-from lines, states and dots, the fold, and the set's details; the Jobs cards; Roles' six rows. Of these, `models:roles` (Chat now `plain`) and `download` (Gemma4-E4B-4bit's note under its actions, behind the sheet) change their frames, shot again after the fixes. ### 2026-11-20 — `settings-and-appearance` (backlog item 1i): Settings, including Appearance

**Why.** Measured at the frozen revision `b375fac9` (8,082 lines): the old Settings window -- the `settings` section of `renderVals()` (6133, 240 lines building `set`) and the `ui.settingsOpen` block (2140, 178 lines) -- drew ten panes over invented data: `provRows`, `healthDefs`, `xcodeRows` and the backend rows were written into the section by hand, while the Mac app's `settings` module (7 files, 2,919 lines) reads the CLI's recorded documents for the same panes. Appearance was the prototype's alone: per-element colors with a hex field, a contrast badge and resets, two fonts, Automatic's two picks. The Keyboard list was a hand-edited copy. 1i ports the window onto the Mac module's three view-models over the stand-in, with Appearance driving 1e's theme service, Keyboard listing the shell's key map, and thirteen presets.

Decisions the item recorded before the build:
- 2026-11-14: **The user's call:** the prototype is rebuilt in React, TypeScript and Vite, with the production GUIs' four layers and MVVM feature modules, built into one self-contained `lib/design/main.html`; a design-queue item in v0.2.1.
- 2026-11-14: **The user's call:** the prototype stays the design's source of truth, so its behavior is ported, not the Mac app's: all 10 panes and the full Appearance pane stay.
- 2026-11-14: **The recorded default:** data comes from the Mac app's recorded fixtures, read in place; a state nobody recorded gets a `*-handmade*` file in the CLI's shape; parity is judged on layout, states, controls and wording, not pixels or data.
- 2026-11-14: The split: Settings is item 1i, gated on 1e; Appearance's state lives in 1e's styles module, and this item drives it.
- (consumed) 1d: where an extracted literal goes and how a new state is recorded; 1e: the Appearance state's shape and the key map; 1h: the roles view the Models & roles pane shows; 1s: how the four new pane presets get their old-prototype frames; 1v: the Streaming session every `settings:*` preset opens over (`streamingRules('start')` composed into its rules, `openStreaming()` in its setup, as `chat:compacting`).

- **`check --fix` is hand-made, though a capture was safe.** Read first: `apply_fixes` writes only inside `APOGEE_HOME` (the data directory's seeding, the starter config at the install's own path, staging left in the store, a scaffolded server's execute bit, `knowledge/raw`'s and the secrets' modes) and starts no vendor binary without `--refresh-providers`. It was not run: a capture would describe a fresh throwaway home -- every `created` line and the report naming its scratch path -- not the presets' recorded world, whose `check` Health shows. So the run prints what `check --fix` prints on that world: `nothing to fix` (an existing install `seed_data_directory` leaves as it is) and `render_report` of its own `check`; the failing run is the same over the failing world, exiting 1 as `run_check_pass` does while a row fails.

### 2026-11-20 — `training-screens` (backlog item 1j): the Training place

**Why.** Measured at the frozen revision `b375fac9` (8,082 lines): the old Training place -- the `training` section of `renderVals()` (6634, 174 lines building `trn`), the `ui.isTraining` markup (1279-1400), the replay helpers `trainRecorded()` through `trainGo()` (3130-3420, with 6i's `trainSweep`, `trainWorld` and `trainResumeAt`), the `promoteRefused` and `train` confirms (6036, 6128) and the `promote` and `pull` sheets (7875, 7932) -- drew two invented runs, three invented regimes, an eight-row kit list and a re-shaped copy of darwin's training streams (`{id, spec, status, student, …}` where the CLI writes `{id, kind, passed, spec_name, …}`), stepped by hand off the clock. The CLI records every one of those documents and streams, and the Mac app's `TrainingViewModel` (1,435 lines) reads them. 1j ports the place onto that view-model's names over the stand-in, with ten presets.

- [x] **The reads** (`business/reads/Training.ts`, the Mac's `Training.swift`): `TrainingRun`, `VersionLedger`, `TrainingStatus` (57g's `stale_lock`, 57h's `cycle_error`, 6i's running ids), `PipelineRunRow`, `PipelineRun` (57h's `resume`, 57g's `holder`, a cycle pass by its id, a score only once gated), `PipelineSpec`, `CycleHeadline`, `CycleHistory`, `TrainingRegime`, `TrainingKit`, `TrainingDataset`.
- [x] **The jobs** (`business/jobs/JobProgress.ts`): the Mac's training parts -- a stage's run and cumulative score (a stage event naming no run keeps the last one's), the pipeline run's id, the trainer's latest iteration, a cycle pass's narration and `pass`.
- [x] **The stand-in's `busy` rule** (`infrastructure/process/StandInChild.ts`, `business/jobs/SupervisedJob.ts`, test-first): an answer's stderr plays a line a tick as the busy line before its stdout, as the Mac samples stderr's last line while a run goes -- `train run` speaks only there, and a promote narrates there before its result. Unless named, stderr stays the tail its end leaves.
- [x] **The `training` module** (`source/presentation/training/`, the stub edited, not deleted):
  - `ViewModels/TrainingViewModel.ts` (1,064 lines): the five tabs; the Mac's reads (`train status`, the cycle only once `train status` says it has a history, `train pipeline list | status`, `train pipeline status <id>` for the open page, `train versions`, `train regime list`, `datasets kits`, `datasets list`); Runs with the promoted versions and Roll back…; the five `train` acts asked first (`Pending`: run a pipeline, resume one, one pass, halt, resume the cycle) and run supervised, one at a time, a pipeline and a pass streamed; the promote form, its refusal and Change the promotion…; the pull, its banner and Prepare it; the prototype's toasts; what the CLI reported kept beside its reads (below); `face()`, everything the views draw.
  - `ViewModels/TrainingForms.ts` (`PromoteForm` with `door(refusal)`, `PulledDataset`), `TrainingWords.ts` (the old prototype's words, the Mac's where the old says none), `TrainingFaces.ts`.
  - `Views/TrainingScreen.tsx` (the header, the five tabs and each tab's buttons, the bar), `RunsView.tsx`, `PipelinesView.tsx` (`PipelinesView`, `StageList`), `CycleView.tsx`, `RegimesView.tsx`, `DatasetsView.tsx` (`DatasetsView`, `PulledBanner`), `TrainingSheets.tsx` (`PromoteSheet`, `PullSheet`, the contents the shell's sheet host frames).

Decisions the item recorded before the build:
- 2026-11-14: **The user's call:** the prototype is rebuilt in React, TypeScript and Vite, in the production apps' four layers and MVVM modules; it stays the design's source of truth and builds to one self-contained `lib/design/main.html`. Release v0.2.1, design track 1.
- 2026-11-14: **The user's call:** parity screenshots are taken by Playwright in 1s, not here.
- 2026-11-14: **The recorded default:** the data is the Mac app's recorded fixtures, read in place; an unrecorded state gets a `*-handmade*` file in the CLI's shape. Parity is judged on layout, states, controls and wording, not pixels or data.
- 2026-11-14: The split: 1j is gated on 1e, and it adds the `training:regimes` preset the old prototype never had.
- (consumed) 1d: the stream replay, the clock and the preset type. 1e: the sheet and confirm hosts and the ⌘9 badge. 1s: the old side of the `training:regimes` pair (1s clicks the tab from `training`) and its gallery board.

The open calls' defaults, taken as stated (2026-11-18, the user's answers before the run):
- **The bare `training` preset reads `train-status.json` only**: one run, 20261021-051826, which failed its gate. The old prototype's second, passed run (20261026-065637, `train-status-passed.json`) is not merged into a hand-made file.
- **The "(prototype)" toasts stay toasts:** Gate it, Run this regime, New dataset…, Synthesize…, Prepare a file… and Delete… say the old words; the Mac's run, synthesize and create sheets are not ported.
- **Rollback stays immediate, as the prototype does** (`train rollback <b>`, its line said); the Mac's ask-first is named, not ported. Every recorded ledger holds one version, so Roll back… is off on every preset, as in the old frames.
- **The Mac-only states are not added** -- **overridden in part by the run's brief (below)** for the two the old prototype now draws (the dead pass's lock and the unreadable history); an older binary's documents are still not drawn.
- **Every preset also runs in the stand-in's `missing` mode** -- **overridden by the run's brief (below)** where the old prototype draws no missing state.

The run's brief (2026-11-18, the user's answers before the run), each for veto:
- **Ten presets, not five** (the scope override): commit `28ff6fe8` gave the old prototype five more Training presets (GUI 6i's, over the 57g/57h recordings): `training:stalelock`, `training:unreadable`, `training:cyclepass`, `training:passrunning` and `training:resumeheld`, with their states (the stale lock, the unreadable history, the cycle-pass page, the hold and the lock wording). 1s fails on any preset left out, so all five are ported here, with the new `training:regimes`: the old nine in the old order, then `training:regimes`. This overrides the document's default that the Mac-only states are not added, for the states the prototype now draws.
- **The missing mode follows the prototype:** "Apogee isn't installed, so there is nothing to train." on Runs and Datasets only, as the old markup draws it (and "Choose a run to see what it trained and how it did.", "Choose a dataset."); Pipelines, Cycle and Regimes have no missing state of their own -- Pipelines says the CLI's no-pipelines lines, Cycle its no-history line and Regimes nothing, as nothing is read -- where the document's default said "Each tab then shows 'Apogee isn't installed'".
- **The hand-made files the document did not list** (the gate refusal it did): a `datasets prepare` run and a successful merged promote (above), the merged promote worded from the source rather than sliced.
- **⌘9 is proven through the key map:** a browser may keep ⌘1-⌘9 for its tabs, so the composition's test presses the shell's map (`shell.press(chord('9', 'command'))`), as 1e's does; the Playwright tests open Training by its sidebar button.
- **The sidebar's badge reads 1h's shared job list**, `placeRuns.training`, never a second path: the old badge counts `running || live` (a run, a pipeline or a pass), so a promote, a pull or a prepare does not count.
- **Nothing trained, pulled or prepared for real, and no capture:** every read and stream is a recording or one of the six hand-made files; no `apogee` binary ran. The npm registry and Playwright's Chromium were allowed; neither was needed (`package.json` and the lock file unchanged; `~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright` still holds `chromium-1248`, `chromium_headless_shell-1248` and `ffmpeg-1013`). Google Fonts was not fetched: 1c's frames were on disk. A Playwright test opening the built file from `file://` stood in for your look; **you try the file afterwards** (Not done).

Decisions taken while building (2026-11-20, an unattended group run), each for veto:
- **Seven worlds** (`TrainingWorldRules.ts`, over `TrainingWorlds.ts`' recordings), each a state of a home the CLI recorded: `recorded` (`train-status.json`, no named pipeline, no cycle: `training`, `training:datasets`, `training:regimes`, and the place on every other preset); `pipelines` (the same with `nightly` and `skills` named, the `skills` run aborting at its second gate, its resume, and the cycle's three passes in the order one home recorded them: skipped, passed, failed); `cycle` (`train-status-cycle.json`, `train-cycle-status-passed.json`, `train-pipeline-status-list.json`'s four pipeline runs -- recorded before the passing pass, whose run `cycle-20261026-074119` its `train status` counts as a fifth, `latest_pipeline`; its next pass the failing one); and GUI 6i's `stalelock` (`training:stalelock` and `training:cyclepass`), `unreadable`, `resumeheld` and `passrunning`. Every world answers the ledgers, the regimes, the kits, the dataset, the pull and the prepare, and New run… and the promotes of the run `train-run.err` recorded (below).
- **`training:cyclepass` and `training:resumeheld` read the stale-lock home** (`train-status-cycle-stale-lock.json`, `train-cycle-status-stale-lock.json`), where the old drew that home's history with no lock: no recording of that home has neither a dead lock nor a live one, so their Cycle tabs show the lock -- off their frames, which open on Pipelines. `training:passrunning` reads 57g's live lock (`train-*-status-live-lock.json`), so its Cycle tab says a pass is running, where the old drew no history.
- **The stand-in keeps no state (1g's rule), so what the CLI reported here is kept beside its reads, as 1h's `land` keeps a pull's sets:** a pipeline run's closing document is its run's manifest (and its row), newer than any read of it; a cycle pass's closing document is the history; and a halt or a resume that exited 0 is applied to the history as the CLI's line says it (halted, with the reason a halt by hand records, `halted by 'apogee train cycle halt'`, as `train-cycle-status-halted-by-hand.json` records it; resumed, the halt and the failure count cleared, as `cycle resumed …; the failure count is reset` says). That resume's line is answered by world: `train-cycle-resume-after-kept.txt` on the `recorded` world (no `training.cycle`, so no breaker trips there: its only halt is one by hand) and on the five 6i worlds; `train-cycle-resume.txt`, the breaker's, on `pipelines` and `cycle`, whose narrated path is the breaker -- so there a resume after a halt by hand says the breaker's line (`train-cycle-resume.txt`) where the CLI would say `train-cycle-resume-after-kept.txt`'s, as the stateless stand-in cannot tell which halt came first (flagged for veto, 2026-11-20). A run this place opened by id that the stand-in's list lacks is listed beside the list's rows. A promote, a rollback and a pull change no read (the ledgers stay as recorded).
- **The cycle's passes replay as one home recorded them**: on `training:pipelines` skipped, passed, then failed (each landing its document, a consistent history); on `training:cycle` the failing one, which trips the breaker. No recording continues either home past that, so a later pass replays the last recording again (the stand-in's list repeats its last answer); the old prototype appended a skipped pass dated now.
- **A halt during a pass keeps the halt** as the CLI's M30 binary does: the view-model assumes today's binary -- a halt taken during a pass is kept, a pull streams -- as the old prototype draws them, and the Mac's older-binary ways (its vocabulary date, `__complete` for the specs) are not ported. A halt taken while this place's pass runs is kept on top of the pass's closing history, the reason by hand standing over the breaker's (`cycle.cpp`: the breaker never trips over a kept halt), as the old's `trainCycleAfter` builds the next history from the halted one; and the pass's end says the CLI's kept notice (`halt_kept_notice`'s words, the old's `kept`) where the replayed stream, recorded with no halt, does not -- a failing pass's end says its failure, as the old's `lv.err` comes first and the Mac says a failed job's message, the notice then on the Cycle tab. The failing pass's row keeps the breaker's note its recording wrote, where the CLI would write none over a kept halt. On a preset, `train cycle halt` answers `train-cycle-halt.txt` whatever runs here (the stand-in cannot tell whether this place's own pass is running), except `training:passrunning`, where a pass always holds the home, which answers the recorded `train-cycle-halt-during-pass.txt` (`the pass running now finishes, and keeps the halt`, the old's toast there); the kept halt is also replayed whole from `train-cycle-run-halted.jsonl` and `train-cycle-halt-during-pass.txt` in its test.
- **New run… trains again what the run shown trained, on its data** -- `train run /Users/someone/work/student --dataset /Users/someone/work/data.jsonl`, 20261021-051826's own recorded facts -- as the old button starts a run at once with no sheet; so on the presets whose run shown is 20261021-051826 (`training`, `training:datasets`, `training:pipelines`, `training:regimes`) the run replayed is the one `train-run.err` recorded. On the others the run shown is a pipeline stage's, which no `train run` recording trains again, so New run… says the stand-in's words there (below). It is held while an act runs here, as the Mac holds it (the old left it enabled, doing nothing: a defect fixed). Likewise Halt… is held with no apogee, as the Mac's `canHaltCycle` holds it (the old left it enabled there), and a pipeline run going here is named `Starting…` -- the old bar's word -- until its stream names its id (the old knew its canned ids in advance).
- **`training` stands at four ticks**, its busy line `train-run.err`'s fourth (`[train] iter 2/4 · loss 0.6200 · lr 1.00e-04 · 10.0 it/s`, the old frame's); `training:pipelines` at 24, the `skills` stream's 24th line (stage 2, iteration 2).
- **The pipeline list is `train-pipeline-list.json`**, the document `train pipeline list --output-format json` prints, where the document named its text twin `train-pipeline-list.txt`.
- **A pipeline run's resume, on a document before 57h, is the bare form** (`apogee train pipeline resume pipe-20261026-074112`, "from the first stage that has not passed", the Mac's rule), as the document's command strings say it, where the old's added `--pipeline skills` and "after the last stage that passed"; a 57h document's resume names its `--pipeline` (`training:resumeheld`'s `--pipeline nightly`).
- **Endings say what the old prototype's toast says, in the CLI's words:** a pipeline's failure its stream's error, a done pipeline `Pipeline <name>: <status>` (the finished run's own manifest's status, whatever page is open), a pass its last narration line (or the kept-halt notice), a failed pass its error, a pull `Pulled: <its count line>`, a refused promote the `promoteRefused` confirm; a Stop the Mac's words (`Stopped: what passed stays -- Resume continues it`, `Stopped: what was finished stays`), since the stand-in's Stop ends a replay before the CLI's own cancel line.
- **Prepare it fails, as the CLI's source says it would** (flagged): `datasets pull` keeps the repository's folders (`acquire_tree` writes each file at its relative path, `data/train.jsonl`), and `prepare_dataset.py`'s `load_source` reads only a folder's top-level files, so `datasets prepare <the pulled folder>` -- the command the pull's own last line names -- ends `No supported data files found in '<folder>'`. The old prototype never drew the outcome (its Prepare it ended in a "(prototype)" toast). The banner stays, and the toast says the CLI's line. A CLI gap, for the CLI's queue (Not done).
- **The promote sheet:** Onto lists the backends the ledgers name (`based`, `q4`, `tuned`), as the old sheet lists them, where the Mac lists the llama.cpp and mlx entries `models list --all` names (that read is not ported); the Judge menu is the old's two choices. Only the refusals recorded for these arguments answer (`exact` rules): unforced, the gate's (merged into `based`, `q4`, `tuned` or `student-tuned`, or the adapter onto `based` or `tuned`); forced, merged into `based` `train-promote-names-adapter.err` and the adapter onto `tuned` `train-promote-holds-merged.err`; run 20261026-065638's adapter onto `q4` with no suite `train-promote-adapter-no-suite.err` (in its test: no preset lists that run); and the forced merge into `student-tuned`, the hand-made success. Any other promote says the stand-in's words. Promote is held while an act runs here (one at a time, the Mac's supervisor).
- **The words:** the old prototype's where it has them -- its straight and curly apostrophes as it writes them ("That didn't work", "your scheduler’s") -- and the Mac's where the old has none (a failed read: `The runs could not be read: <the CLI's line>` in `config-typo`; "Choose a pipeline run…" stays the old's).
- **`TrainingViewModel` keeps the Mac's name and split** -- one view-model, its forms, words and faces in sibling files -- and `Pending` keeps the five `train` acts (rollback is never asked).
- **The document's own decisions were recorded here, not in it:** it is deleted in the same pass.

Decisions taken while fixing the review's defects (2026-11-20, the same unattended run), each for veto:
- **Where a world offers an act no recording answers, the docs say so; no rule replays another id's recording.** New run… on `training:cycle` and the five 6i presets (the run shown is a pipeline stage's: `train run <its fused folder> --dataset <its data>`, which no recording holds), Promote… of those runs, Run… on a named pipeline outside `training:pipelines`, and the resume of `training:cycle`'s aborted `pipe-20261026-074117` are offered as the old offers them and end in the stand-in's `no recorded answer for apogee …`, where the old played a canned run. The review offered hand-made files re-worded from `train-run.err` and `train-pipeline-resume.jsonl` for some of them; not made, since others (the promotes, the runs) would stay unanswered anyway and each would be a recording's ids swapped, not a state the CLI's source words. Listed under Not done.
- **A cycle pass's pipeline run joins no list:** its stream closes on the cycle's history, not a manifest, and the stand-in's `train pipeline status` never changes, so after a pass RUNS has no row for `cycle-20261026-074119` or `cycle-20261026-074120`, where the old's `trainEnd` kept one (`trainKeep`, its student and spec written in). Building a manifest from the stream would invent its student and base and read its spec from a narration line; the CLI's next read would list it. On `training:cycle` RUNS is `train-pipeline-status-list.json`'s four runs, though its `train status` says five (above). Listed under Not done.
- **The fixture check reads the rules without the recordings:** `TrainingWorlds.ts` was split, its `?raw` imports and `recording()` kept, its worlds moved to `TrainingWorldRules.ts` (re-exported, so no caller changed), so a recording missing on disk fails `TrainingFixtures.test.ts` once per preset naming it, where before the suite failed at load naming only the file.
- **The two `StageList` previews are built through the view-model** over the `pipelines` world (the `skills` run at stage 2, iteration 2; and `train-pipeline-status-aborted.json`'s manifest), as the Mac's two stage previews are, where they passed hand-typed faces, one a failed-then-pending pairing no recording holds.

**Verified.**
- **Test first.** Watched failing before their code: `Training.test.ts` (its module missing), the three new `JobProgress` tests and the three `busy` tests in `SupervisedJob.test.ts` (5 of 6 failing: one pinned the rule's absence and passed at once); `TrainingViewModel.test.ts`' 23 (against the stub: `training.connect is not a function`), then 3 failed on the tests' own walks (two tests had not picked the Cycle tab, one expected a registry label the registry cuts at the first option) and were corrected; the composition's 5 new tests and the 4 changed `placeRuns` and placeholder expectations (9 failing before the wiring). After the first full gate, two more expectations were watched failing before their code: Halt… held with no apogee, and the live run's `Starting…` before its stream names it. Written after their code and passing at once: the per-preset walk's extension (the presets were in the table before the walk named them), the fixture check (`TrainingFixtures.test.ts`, proven on a planted unimported name, a planted missing file and a planted design fixture not named hand-made), the planted layering violations (pins of 1b's rules), and the Playwright tests (15 new, and 1e's places test changed for Training's tabs; on their first run one failed on its own locator, `running here` matching the live page's sentence too). The review's fixes, each watched failing before its code (6 of 6, `TrainingViewModel.test.ts`): a halt taken during the skipped, the passed and (on `training:cycle`'s world) the failing pass kept when the pass ends, with the kept notice (STATE went back to idle, Halt… offered again; on the failing pass the breaker's reason replaced the one by hand); a resume after a halt by hand on the `recorded` world saying `train-cycle-resume-after-kept.txt`'s line (it said the breaker's); Halt… on `training:passrunning` saying the during-pass line (it said the plain halt's); and `nightly`'s end, with the aborted `skills` run's page open, saying `Pipeline nightly: complete` (it said `aborted`). The fixture check was pointed at `TrainingWorldRules.ts` first and failed on the missing module.
- **The guards have teeth.** A mutation pass in a `git clone --shared` under the session scratchpad (sparse: `lib/design` and `lib/src/darwin/tests`), the working copy laid over it, `node_modules` linked, nothing set in `GIT_*`, ran 22 mutants against the Training, jobs and composition tests: the `busy` rule ignored, a stage's run not carried, the iteration and the narration dropped, the holder ignored, the stale lock not holding Run one pass, a halt and a resume not applied, a pass's and a run's documents not landed, the refusal's door keeping its mode, a refusal only said, a running cycle pass said discarded, a resume dropping `--pipeline`, the pull not streamed, a halt allowed on an unreadable history, the badge counting a pull, the place's reads off every other preset, New run… naming the dataset, a resumed stage's old score shown before its gate, a missing read said as read, and the merged promote's fixture dropped. 20 failed a test; of the 2 survivors, the score shown before its gate is now caught by the resume test reading the live stage mid-resume (its rerun failed: 21 of 21), and the other was dead code (`listingText`'s missing branch, which the faces never reach), removed. The clone was removed after. After the review's fixes, a second `git clone --shared` (sparse the same, the working copy laid over it) had `train-cycle-status-stale-lock.json` deleted: `TrainingFixtures.test.ts` failed `training:stalelock`, `training:cyclepass` and `training:resumeheld`, each `… is missing at <path>`, the other nine passing (before the split, the file failed at load with no test run). That clone was removed after too.
- **Every preview draws:** the 43 Training previews rendered server-side without a throw and with no `undefined` or `NaN`, in a throwaway test deleted after; after the review's fixes, the two rebuilt `StageList` previews' view-models, set up as `useTrainingPreview` sets them, gave `greetings` `passed · cumulative 100%` and `refusals` `training · iter 2/4 · loss 0.6200 · 10.0 it/s` (`Stages`) and `failed · cumulative 50%` (`StagesFailed`), and `StageList` rendered them with no `undefined` or `NaN` (a throwaway test in the clone).
- **`npm run check`**, exit 0, on the final tree:
  - `tsc --noEmit` clean;
  - `unit` 1,106 of 1,106 in 73 files (was 956 in 71; 1,098 before the review's fixes, which added their 6 tests, the unknown-recording refusal moved out of the fixture check, and `Replays.test.ts`' provenance test for the newly imported `train-cycle-halt-during-pass.txt`), the per-preset walk 48 presets × 3 modes, still and after 30 ticks, with the Training place read on every one and its face, sheets and both confirms' words in what the views read, both stand-in children's `unanswered` empty;
  - `layering` 61 of 61 in 2 files (was 60), the token scan among them;

### 2026-11-21 — `video-and-runtime-consent` (backlog item 1k): the Video place's clips and the runtime consent

**Why.** Measured at the frozen revision `b375fac9` (8,082 lines): the old Video place's clips -- the "video and voice" helper group (4482: `framesOf`, `clipName`, `freshClipForm`, `formFrom`, `clipErrors`, `buildRun`, `vrunTiming`, `vrunAt`, `startGenerate`), "media: shared" (6982, 46 lines: `player`, `seek`, `phaseRow`), "video: clips" (7028, 104), "video: the generate form" (7132, 148) and "runtime consent" (7740, 38: `rtc`, `inst`), the markup `ui.isVideo` (1610, `isClips` 1621) and `ui.runtimeOpen` (2453), and two `tick()` steps -- drew five invented clips (no CLI-shaped key), a run stepped by hand off the clock, and a consent raised by a client-side `runtimes.peft` flag. The CLI writes a `video.generation` record beside every clip, refuses a LoRA without peft in its own words (`video_generate.cpp:696`), and the Mac app's `VideoViewModel` (410 lines), `ClipForm`, `InstallConsent` and `RuntimeInstall` read them. 1k ports the place onto those names over the stand-in, with four presets, and moves the player, the phase row, the install card and the consent sheet into `styles` for Compose, Voice and Settings.

- [x] **`business/media`** (new, the Mac's `BusinessMedia`; `modules.json`: on `process` and `protocol`): `MediaRecords.ts` -- `ClipRecord` (the record whole: the model and its class, the prompt, the image, the mode, the seed and its source, the settings with frames and guidance, the codec, the seconds, the parent's move, record, clip and continued clip, the preset, each LoRA with its sha256, the device, the engine, the versions) and `MediaLibrary` (`scan` and `clips`: a shallow walk for `*.json`, the object matched, a record whose clip is gone passed over and said, newest first; `chain`, `freeName`, `stem`, `installCommand`, `rebuilds`, `rebuildNote`); `ClipForm.ts` (the Mac's, its argument order the verdicts'); `MediaEngine.ts` (`MediaEngine`, `MediaModel`: `videoModels` from `models list`, `offered`, `choosesEngine`, `label`, `named`); `InstallConsent.ts` (`fromRun` -- the stream's `error` first, else the failure and stderr -- `fromFailure`, `afterFailed`, `waits`, `heldBack`, `command`, `set`).

Decisions the item recorded before the build:
- 2026-11-14: **The user's call:** the prototype is rebuilt in React and TypeScript with the production GUIs' four layers and MVVM modules, so it stays the design's cross-platform source of truth.
- 2026-11-14: **The recorded default:** data comes from the Mac app's recorded CLI fixtures, read in place; a state nobody recorded gets a hand-made file in the CLI's exact shape, named `*-handmade*`.
- 2026-11-14: **The recorded default:** parity is judged on layout, states, controls and wording, not pixels or data.
- 2026-11-14: **The split:** Video and the consent are one item, because the consent is raised from the generate form and its install card is drawn in the clips list; it moves the player to `styles`, so 1l and 1m wait on it.
- (consumed) 1d: the stand-in's job, run-sequence and preset types, and where hand-made files live. 1e: the `styles` module and the two media tokens. 1l: the compositions tab's content. 1q: the ComponentsMedia board, drawn from these real components. 1s: the parity verdicts.

The open calls' defaults, taken as stated (2026-11-18, the user's answers before the run):
- **The player plays off the stand-in's clock** at the prototype's rate (0.4 s of clip a tick) and freezes under `still`; it decodes no real video and draws the prototype's frame (`Playback`, `MediaPlayer`).
- **The install's first try fails and the next succeeds**, as in the prototype: the stand-in serves `install-video-failed.*`, then `install-video-done.txt`, then the refused generate runs again. Between the two, as the old flow has it, the person generates again and the CLI refuses again (below).
- **Until 1l lands, the app root fills the compositions slot with a plain placeholder** (`CompositionsPlaceholder`: "The compositions. Design item 1l draws them here.").
- **The run is a job in the stand-in's jobs service, not view-model state:** the shared job list holds it, so the sidebar's Video badge (`placeRuns.video`) and the monitor read it there. **Models shows nothing of it:** the old Models tab drew a media model's "generating" status only on a backend row its `inTab` never draws (media models are filtered out), so no place already built had anything more to show.
- **Settings' Install… opens the same sheet** -- this item wires it, 1i having landed first: the old "Install peft…" on the `Video · diffusers` row became Install… on the video runtime's row (above).

The run's brief (2026-11-18, the user's answers before the run), each for veto:
- **The network was not used.** The npm registry and Playwright's Chromium were allowed and not needed (`package.json` and the lock file unchanged; `~/Library/Caches/ms-playwright` still holds `chromium-1248`, `chromium_headless_shell-1248` and `ffmpeg-1013`); Google Fonts was not fetched (1c's frames were on disk). No `apogee` binary ran, and nothing installed or generated for real: every stream is a recording or hand-made.
- **A Playwright test opening the built file from `file://` stood in for your look**; you try the file afterwards (Not done).
- **The path is `source/presentation/video/`**, where the document's seam said `src/`: the tree and the layering check use `source/`.
- **The command test conflict:** the run's argument list is the Mac's `ClipForm`, in the order `engine-command-verdicts.txt`'s "taken" lines record (`-o OUT -m M --engine E -- prompt`), held to all six of them by the command test; the drawn command keeps the prototype's words (below).

Decisions taken while building (2026-11-20 and 2026-11-21, an unattended group run), each for veto:
- **The clip records come from the folders the CLI writes into, not a read** (flagged): no CLI verb lists clips -- `video` has `generate` and `compose` only, and `media-list.json` is a `models list` of the store's media models -- and the Mac app scans `~/Movies/Apogee` for the `video.generation` records `video generate` writes beside each clip (the document its `--output-format json` prints and its stream closes on). So a preset hands the stand-in those folders' files (`Preset.folders`, `StandInFolders`, in `process` beside `StandInChild`; no new module), and `MediaLibrary.scan` reads them as the Mac reads them. Every preset carries `~/Movies/Apogee`'s five records. 1l reads `video.project` records from the same folder through the same scan; 1m reads `voice.speech` records from `~/Music/Apogee`, a second folder in the table (DEVELOPER.md).
- **`RuntimeInstall` and the media progress live in `business/jobs`**, where the Mac keeps them (`BusinessJobs`), not `business/media` as the document's seam listed: ADR 0001 §1 has a file's path say which Swift module it mirrors.
- **The drawn command keeps the prototype's words; the run is the CLI's verdict order** (flagged): the command under the form and on a clip's page is the old one -- the prompt first and quoted, `--model`, `-o` last, paths under `~/` -- and the job runs `ClipForm`'s argument list with absolute paths. The CLI's parser takes either order, and the verdicts record no prompt-first line either way, so nothing refuses the drawn one; a test holds both to the same flags and values. `--engine` joins both where the form's model's folder holds a pipeline beside its MLX conversion (the old form had no engine; the recorded store has both for Wan 2.2), as the Mac passes it.
- **The default pipeline is the CLI's `models.default_video`** (`media-default-video.txt`: the MLX conversion), and the hand-made clips ran on the Wan 2.2 pipeline (`clip-torch.json`'s model) on PyTorch, so rain's LoRA reaches the peft refusal (PyTorch only; mlx-video loads a LoRA itself). The header says `Wan2.2-TI2V-5B · MLX · default`.
- **The video world's store** (flagged): `models-list-video-handmade.json`, Settings' world list with `media-list.json`'s Wan 2.2 pipeline row, so rain's record names a model the store holds; and that pipeline's `models info`, hand-made from `render_stored_pipeline` over the row's own facts (4 components, 2 shards, 462 B, no record), its per-component files and bytes chosen to sum to the row's 462 B -- the Models place reads every listed set's info. The Wan 2.1 pipeline is left out, as 1i left both out, since its info would be one more invention. On any other preset the place reads that preset's list; where it names no video model the form's pipeline is not checked against the store, and the CLI answers.
- **The told box is the CLI's own words** (`seed_run` ported to `VideoForm.told`): what the run takes from its record and what changed, a record from another platform, a preset's steps -- absolute paths, as the CLI says them, where the old drew its own summary. It is drawn for a record run again or a clip continued, as the old form draws it; a new clip's `preset: draft -- 16 steps` is the run's to say. The hand-made streams' notices are what `told` computes for their forms (a test holds them equal).
- **What the run says is its stream:** the CLI's notices in order, then its text mode's narration -- the runtime loaded, a step at every tenth of the way and the last (`step 1/50`, `5/50`, …), the frames written, their assembly -- where the old printed `step 10/50`, `20/50`, … after its own summary line. A Stop ends with the CLI's cancel line (`cancelled -- nothing was written (<clip> untouched)`), since the stand-in's Stop ends a replay before the CLI's own.
- **A refusal that names an install leaves no run drawn:** the CLI refused before it ran anything, so the place asks the consent and draws no failed run; the old raised the consent before any run from its `runtimes.peft` flag.
- **The consent says the stream's error**, `a LoRA loads through peft, which the video runtime does not have yet -- apogee train setup --with video`, as the Mac's `InstallConsent` reads it first since the CLI's M29; the old drew stderr's form, with `apogee video generate: ` before it. Both are the CLI's words.
- **Rain run again is refused until the install has gone in** (flagged): the stand-in's `until` (above) answers rain's generate with the CLI's refusal, uncounted, until `train setup --with video` has been answered with a success and not stopped -- however often Generate is pressed, and whoever ran the install (the video place, or Settings' Install…) -- then with the made clip. So the old flow holds (the first install fails, Generate is refused again, the second goes in and the clip is made) and `consent`, `video:install` and the live click-through all reach the clip. `video:install` runs the real refused Generate, then Install once its consent asks (the composition's `afterStart`), so its frame is the CLI's run, never a state set by hand; each install's end is held to the old 11 ticks by `exitAfter`, the one that goes in resting on its `installing …` line (`holdAt`) until its end, as the old card does. (Built first as refused, refused, made by call count; fixed after review, 2026-11-21: below.)
- **The hand-made records** (flagged): the old five clips in the CLI's shape, keyed as `clip-mlx.json`. Their times are two hours earlier than the old's (Today 11:12, 10:58, 10:41; Yesterday 18:20; Oct 16) so a run made at the stand-in's fixed now (12:00) lists first; `generated` is the CLI's word for the old `drawn`; the extension names `final`, passed with `--extend`; the class is the index's (`WanPipeline`, where the old drew `WanImageToVideoPipeline` for rain); the versions are what the CLI records (torch, diffusers, peft, apogee -- no transformers); the codec `h264`; rain's sha256 a full hash ending as the old's abbreviation does.
- **A record's facts say what it records:** Versions led by the engine (`PyTorch · diffusers 0.35.1 · torch 2.8.0`, the Mac's `said(versions:)`, sorted); Codec as recorded; Files with no sizes (`the clip`, `the record`: the CLI reports none, where the old drew `8.7 MB`); a seed image with no size (the old's `1280×720`); an absent field said in words (`not recorded`), never guessed.
- **The form's Memory row says only what the store says** (`Its weights are 462 B on disk, as the store counts them.`, or that it does not say): the old's free memory and loaded model were invented numbers, and its Unload link is gone. **Runs on** keeps the old words for PyTorch and says mlx-video for MLX.
- **A seed of its own** (Re-roll, Give one) is drawn from the clock, not `Math.random`, so a frame draws the same one twice; **Add a seed image…** and **Add a LoRA file…** offer the old samples (a browser picks no file), an added LoRA's hash said "checked by its sha256 when the run starts", not invented.
- **The Compositions tab draws no count** until 1l reads its records (the old drew 4), and New composition is off there until 1l wires it.
- **The monitor lists a video run** from the shared job list with Stop the run and no size (the old's 23.6 GB was invented), through closures, so the monitor takes no `jobs` edge: the place's own clip once its pipeline has loaded, as the old lists `vrun` past `load` (any other generate -- one typed in the console -- by its label, as the badge counts it), named by the store's folder (`Wan2.2-TI2V-5B · generating`, the old's display name `Wan 2.2 TI2V-5B` being invented) with the device and type its `ready` said (`a video run this app supervises · mps · bfloat16 · the CLI reports no size for it`, where the old said `video child process · mps · bfloat16 · measured`).
- **InstallCard is promoted with no second user yet** (flagged, as the brief asked): Settings draws no install card (1i drew none), and Compose and Voice draw no install by their recorded defaults, so no queued item is its second user; the acceptance criterion alone puts it in `styles`.
- **Install… on the video runtime's row only:** the voice runtime's row (`apogee train setup --with voice`) gets none: no queued item ports a voice install (voice-screen.md keeps it Mac-only). Settings' consent says the row in `check`'s words (`Video · runtime: not set up -- torch is not installed …`), so Settings and Video each say their own recording: the stand-in keeps no state, and an install that went in changes no `check` row.
- **Install waits only on another install** (flagged; 2026-11-21, after review): Install starts nothing while an install runs -- this place's own, said in its words (`apogee train setup --with video is already running: press Install again once it has finished`), or another place's bootstrap -- and keeps the consent; a rebuild waits on a run on the environment too, this place's clip among them. A clip being made is no install, so Install from Settings starts beside it: the document's rule (only another install blocks, saying why) and the old `rtc.install()`, which checks for a bootstrap alone. Two review findings asked for this and for the run to block too; the document's rule decided between them.
- **A run's phases say its own plan:** its steps and frames are taken from the form as it starts, so editing the form while it runs changes nothing its Denoise and Assemble say; a refused run is kept whole -- its prompt, model and plan -- and started again once the install is in (where the build looked its record up by `--from`, so a new clip ran again with an empty prompt and pipeline).
- **A continuation is read by the CLI's own `continued`**, as `VideoForm.told` reads it: an extension, and a record run again from one (which the CLI writes as `move: from` with `continued`, `video_generate_test.cpp:553`), say `continues <clip>`, Made says the last frame it came from, and its form continues with no seed image. No hand-made record is such a clip; a test builds one.
- **The file field shows what was typed:** emptied, it is empty, the form says `Name the file to write.` and the command ends `-o ~/Movies/Apogee/…`, as the old `outName` does; the command and the run take the name trimmed.
- **The stub's view and preview were written anew at their paths** (`Views/VideoScreen.tsx`, `Previews/VideoScreen.preview.tsx`), its view-model and test edited; nothing else deleted.
- **The document's own decisions were recorded here, not in it:** it is deleted in the same pass.

**Side by side with the frozen old prototype** (1c's four frames -- `video`, `video:run`, `video:install` and `consent` at `installed` -- against Playwright shots of the built file at 1440 × 900, still). Every region, control and state is drawn: the header's title, tabs, pipeline line and New clip; the clips list with its tags, metas and foot; the install card with its bar and Stop; the run row; a clip's page with its preset tag, mode, prompt, the player paused with its time and bar, the four or five actions, the other-platform note, RECORD's twelve facts, LORAS, CHAIN with its links, FILES and the command; the run with Stop, its three phases and WHAT THE RUN SAYS; the form's heading, Start fresh, told box, prompt, start, pipeline, quality, seconds, rate, steps, size, frames, seed, LoRAs, file, errors, facts, command and Generate; and the consent sheet with Not now and Install. The differences, each intended (the CLI's data) or a defect fixed:
- **Everywhere:** the sidebar's chat glyphs and the SYSTEM strip are 1g's and 1e's; the Streaming session behind is 1v's; the Compositions tab has no count (above); the pipeline line names the CLI's default handle and engine; the list's times are the hand-made records' (above).
- **`video`:** the facts in the CLI's words (the model handle and its class, `PyTorch · diffusers 0.35.1 · torch 2.8.0`, `h264`); the form's pipeline `Wan2.2-TI2V-5B · MLX · default`. A told box under New clip -- `preset: draft -- 16 steps` -- was drawn where the old drew none: **a defect, fixed** (drawn for a record run again or continued only).
